1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + plan ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **plan ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	
256	### Body
257	
258	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
259	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
260	
261	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
262	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
263	  files with multiple distinct changes:
264	
265	  ```
266	  - path/to/file1
267	    - first distinct change
268	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
269	       with continuation indented 5
270	  ```
271	
272	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
273	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
274	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
275	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
276	
277	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
278	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
279	
280	### Trailer
281	
282	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
283	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
284	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
285	
286	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
287	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
288	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
289	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
290	
291	## Reviewing changes
292	
293	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
294	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
295	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
296	
297	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
298	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
299	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
300	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
301	
302	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
303	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
304	`jj show -r @-`.
305	
306	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
307	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
308	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
309	
310	## Pushing
311	
312	### Policy
313	
314	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
315	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
316	the cycle's result must be published.
317	
318	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
319	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
320	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
321	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
322	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
323	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
324	
325	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
326	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
327	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
328	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
329	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
330	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
331	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
332	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
333	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
334	
335	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
336	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
337	  completion.
338	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
339	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
340	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
341	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
342	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
343	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
344	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
345	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
346	  reaches this.
347	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
348	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
349	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
350	  per-push approval.
351	
352	### Shape at close-out push
353	
354	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
355	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
356	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
357	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
358	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
359	
360	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
361	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
362	  focused on one or two files.
363	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
364	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
365	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
366	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
367	  for the full setup sequence.
368	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
369	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
370	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
371	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
372	
373	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
374	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
375	
376	### Merge non-ff recipe
377	
378	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
379	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
380	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
381	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
382	Work commit:
383	
384	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
385	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
386	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
387	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
388	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
389	
390	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
391	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
392	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
393	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
394	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
395	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
396	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
397	
398	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
399	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
400	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
401	push force-updates `main`.
402	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
403	the close-out push.
404	
405	### vc-x1 push wrapper
406	
407	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
408	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
409	
410	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
412	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
413	planned:
414	
415	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
416	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
417	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
418	
419	### .claude cadence
420	
421	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
422	with every code commit in that push.
423	
424	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
425	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
426	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
427	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
428	
429	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
430	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
431	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
432	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
433	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
434	
435	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
436	session information created, which we don't worry about.
437	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
438	remain empty.
439	
440	### Bot communication at the reviews
441	
442	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
443	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
444	
445	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
446	  "Work complete. Please review."
447	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
448	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
449	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
450	  --body ...` invocation by default.
451	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
452	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
453	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
454	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
455	
456	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
457	
458	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
459	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
460	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
461	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
462	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
463	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
464	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
465	stop for the whole turn.
466	
467	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
468	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
469	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
470	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
471	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
472	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
473	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
474	
475	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
476	squashes → pushes):
477	
478	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
479	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
480	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
481	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
482	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
483	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
484	  the bot to do more work.
485	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
486	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
487	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
488	  never a summary or a next step.
489	
490	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
491	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
492	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
493	
494	### Recovery
495	
496	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
497	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
498	  finalize by hand:
499	
500	  ```
501	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
502	  ```
503	
504	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
505	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
506	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
507	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
508	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
509	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
510	    *after* the snapshot.
511	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
512	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
513	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
514	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
515	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
516	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
517	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
518	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
519	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
520	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
521	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
522	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
523	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
524	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
525	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
526	  stale stage.
527	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
528	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
529	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
530	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
531	  push.
532	
533	## Iterative work
534	
535	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
536	from incremental review, loop:
537	
538	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
539	2. Make the next round of changes.
540	3. User reviews the round (see
541	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
542	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
543	   new empty `@`.
544	5. If not done, go to step 2.
545	
546	Same jj mechanics as a
547	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
548	single-commit scope — the version
549	doesn't change.
550	
551	## Sub-cycle ladders
552	
553	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
554	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
555	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
556	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
557	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
558	for review and bisection only.
559	
560	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
561	
562	For each Work commit in the ladder:
563	
564	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
565	2. Do the commit's work.
566	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
567	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
568	   alone miss regressions until a later commit runs the full
569	   suite, raising bisection cost.
570	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
571	   only; the sub-cycle Close-out collects everything
572	   into one final commit.
573	
574	### Navigating the ladder
575	
576	Common moves:
577	
578	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
579	  from its base.
580	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
581	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
582	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
583	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
584	  isolation.
585	
586	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
587	descendants auto-rebase.
588	
589	### Close-out: squash the ladder
590	
591	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
592	
593	```
594	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
595	```
596	
597	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
598	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
599	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
600	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
601	
602	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
603	
604	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
605	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
606	
607	### Recovery
608	
609	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
610	prior commits:
611	
612	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
613	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
614	  parent.
615	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
616	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
617	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
618	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
619	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
620	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
621	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
622	
623	# References
624	
625	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
626	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
627	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
628	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
629	  for squash-recipe experiments.
630	- The per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate exists because a
631	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
632	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
633	  bisection cost.
634	
```

1	# Versioning
2	
3	How this project versions its commits and the running
4	artifact. The scheme — the cycle step-numbering and the
5	unique-per-commit aim — is generic and shared across projects:
6	this file is copied **verbatim**, with
7	[Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
8	covering each medium by conditional rather than per-project
9	edits.
10	
11	## Terms
12	
13	Three names, used as defined here across
14	[AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md),
15	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md), and the notes files:
16	
17	- **version** — the per-commit version (e.g. `0.3.0-5.3.0`),
18	  written with `-` in prose, ladders, chores, todo, and commit
19	  talk; its suffix encodes the cycle phase (see
20	  [Step numbering](#step-numbering)).
21	- **version-of-record** — the authoritative stored copy of the
22	  version, in the project's manifest (see
23	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record));
24	  a built or running artifact derives from it.
25	- **versioning** — the topic: this scheme as a whole.
26	
27	## Recording the version-of-record
28	
29	Where the version-of-record lives, how it's stored and
30	surfaced, and how often it changes — pick the case that fits
31	your medium:
32	
33	- **Manifest** — where the version-of-record is stored:
34	  - if Rust, `Cargo.toml` `[package].version`
35	  - if Python, `pyproject.toml` `[project].version` (or the
36	    committed config it's sourced from)
37	  - otherwise wherever the medium records it (a generic
38	    `version.toml`, a book's frontmatter, …) — add the case as
39	    needed
40	- **Notation** — how the `-` form is stored:
41	  - if the format allows `-` (TOML `version.toml`, `Cargo.toml`),
42	    store it verbatim
43	  - if it bars `-` (PEP 440's local segment, e.g. a Python
44	    project), remap to `+` — `0.3.0-5.3.0` → `0.3.0+5.3.0`: same
45	    version, just the stored spelling
46	- **Reporter** — how a built artifact surfaces the
47	  version-of-record:
48	  - if a CLI app, `<cli-app> -V`
49	  - if a TUI/GUI, add to Help/About or display on the title
50	- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
51	  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
52	  this project follows the per-commit preference.
53	
54	## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)
55	
56	Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
57	on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
58	the exact commit it came from.
59	
60	- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
61	  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
62	  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
63	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
64	  if it differs.
65	- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
66	  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
67	  contain (see the cycle protocol's
68	  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).
69	
70	## Step numbering
71	
72	The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
73	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
74	phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
75	a Preparation**:
76	
77	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
78	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
79	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
80	
81	**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
82	no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
83	Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
84	needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
85	grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
86	renumbering siblings.
87	
88	Disambiguation:
89	
90	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
91	  Preparation.
92	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
93	  identifier `0`).
94	
95	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
96	
97	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
98	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
99	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
100	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
101	
102	Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
103	active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
104	every commit carries a distinct version.
105	
```

```
vc-x1 0.66.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

Two things to confirm:
1. Title is 42 chars, fine — but note the chores header stays `docs: zero-copy ring buffer design` (the anticipated *close-out* title), which is correct per protocol.

OK to commit and push this one?

> DEVELOPER

Hmm, the chore title is "docs: zero-copy ring buffer design" no "Pick up" also change "pick up todo," in -0 to "update todo," and since each step will have the same title let's append the sub-step as "(ss-0)" so each will be unique. Thoughts.

> AGENT

Good catches on both — the ladder `-0` line should say "update todo," and I'll fix that. On the shared-title-plus-`(ss-N)` idea, here are my thoughts:

**What the protocol currently expects:** each step gets its *own* conventional-commit title (e.g. `docs: ring buffer requirements + constraints` for `-1`), and only the **close-out** commit's title matches the chores header verbatim. The identical-title rule ties one step's three records together (its ladder line, its chores header, its commit) — it doesn't ask all steps in a cycle to share a title. So uniqueness is already solved: distinct titles per step, and the version-of-record (`0.3.0-N` in version.toml) pins each commit's step.

**Your proposal** (all steps titled `docs: zero-copy ring buffer design (ss-0)`, `(ss-1)`, …) works and has a real benefit — `git log --grep "zero-copy ring buffer design"` returns the whole cycle. Two costs:

- It rubs against the "titles carry no trailing suffix" rule. That rule's stated rationale (versions unstable pre-merge) barely applies to sub-steps, but a `(ss-N)` is the same shape and has the same fragility: insert or reorder a step and the numbering shifts, requiring rewrites of already-committed titles or tolerating drift.
- Uniform titles say less in `jj log` — you can't see what a commit did without opening it.

**My recommendation:** keep distinct per-step titles (protocol default), all sharing a common greppable stem, e.g. `docs: ring buffer requirements`, `docs: ring buffer API + layout design`, `feat: ring buffer prototype crate` — "ring buffer" grep still collects the cycle, titles stay informative, no numbering to maintain. But if you prefer the uniform `(ss-N)` scheme, it's workable — I'd then record the convention in AGENTS.md so future cycles follow it consistently.

Which way do you want to go?

> DEVELOPER

Let's go with your recommendation, distinct per-step titles. But let's fix  AGENTS/cycle-protocol now too.

> AGENT

I'll make the edits: the ladder wording fix, plus the per-step-title clarification in AGENTS.md and cycle-protocol.md.

> DEVELOPER

Before committing above you said "1. Title is 42 chars, fine — but note the chores header stays docs: zero-copy ring buffer design (the anticipated close-out title), which is correct per protocol." We're now changing the "protocol" so we need to modify that statment, do you agree?

> AGENT

Partly agree, mostly not — let me separate the two threads:

**The chores-header statement itself still holds.** The change we just made doesn't alter that rule — it *reaffirms* it. The new AGENTS.md text says explicitly: "The cycle's chores section header carries the anticipated *close-out* title." So `## docs: zero-copy ring buffer design` staying as the chores header while the `-0` commit is titled `docs: pick up zero-copy ring buffer design` is exactly what the updated protocol prescribes. What we changed was the misreading the header invited — that all steps share that title — not the header rule. (And the `-0` title complies with the new stem rule: it contains "ring buffer".)

**But your instinct that something is inconsistent is right — it's the ladder lines.** The Conventional-commit shape rule says a step's ladder entry, chores header, and commit title line up *verbatim* per step. Our ladder currently has informal descriptions ("update todo, open chores, bump version"), not the commit titles. Strictly applied, the ladder should read:

```
- 0.3.0-0 docs: pick up zero-copy ring buffer design (current)
- 0.3.0-1 docs: ring buffer requirements + constraints
- 0.3.0-2 docs: ring buffer API + memory-layout design
- 0.3.0-3 feat: ring buffer prototype crate
- 0.3.0 docs: zero-copy ring buffer design
```

with the later titles being anticipated (provisional until each step commits, synced as the last edit before its push — the protocol already allows that).

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/ring-buffer-design.md",
  "content": "# Ring buffer design\n\nDesign notes for the zero-copy ring buffer. This file uses\n[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). This step covers\nrequirements and constraints; the API and memory-layout design\nfollows in the next cycle step.\n\n## Goal\n\nA ring buffer for passing typed messages between a producer and\na consumer with no copying at the boundary: the producer writes\nits message directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads it\nin place. The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and\ninter-process (shared memory).\n\n## Requirements\n\n- **no_std** — the crate builds with `#![no_std]` and no\n  `alloc`: fixed capacity, operating over a caller-provided\n  memory region. Suitable for embedded and kernel-adjacent use.\n- **Zero-copy via zerocopy** — messages are structs implementing\n  the [zerocopy](https://docs.rs/zerocopy) traits\n  (`IntoBytes`, `FromBytes`, `KnownLayout`, `Immutable`); the\n  buffer transmutes slots into and out of `&T`/`&mut T` with no\n  serialization step and no `unsafe` at the call site.\n- **IPC-suitable layout** — the control block (head/tail\n  indices) and the slot array live in one contiguous region that\n  can be mapped into multiple address spaces:\n  - `#[repr(C)]` explicit layout, stable across processes\n    compiled from the same source.\n  - No pointers inside the region — offsets and indices only.\n- **SPSC first** — single producer, single consumer, lock-free\n  via atomic head/tail. MPMC is out of scope for this cycle;\n  the layout should not preclude adding it later.\n- **In-place access API** — reserve/commit on the producer side\n  and peek/release on the consumer side, exposing references\n  into the buffer rather than returning values by move.\n- **Safety** — all `unsafe` encapsulated inside the crate with\n  documented invariants; public API is safe.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- **Capacity is a power of two** — index wrapping is a mask,\n  no division; checked at construction.\n- **Monotonic indices** — head/tail are free-running unsigned\n  counters (wrap via masking), so full/empty are\n  distinguishable without a separate flag or a sacrificial\n  slot.\n- **Cache-line separation** — producer- and consumer-owned\n  indices are padded/aligned to separate cache lines to avoid\n  false sharing.\n- **Fixed-size slots** — this design is `RingBuffer<T>` with\n  one `T` per slot. Variable-length framed messages are a\n  possible follow-on, not in this cycle.\n- **zerocopy, core-only** — depend on zerocopy with default\n  features compatible with `no_std`; no other runtime\n  dependencies.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- Atomic width for indices: `AtomicU32` vs `AtomicUsize` —\n  cross-process mapping between 32/64-bit peers argues for a\n  fixed-width `AtomicU32`. Decide in the API/layout step.\n- Whether the consumer releases slots one-at-a-time or in\n  batches (batch release amortizes the atomic store).\n- How construction over foreign memory (an mmap'd region) is\n  expressed safely — likely a `from_bytes`-style validated\n  cast via zerocopy's `KnownLayout`.\n"
}
```

Work for `0.3.0-1` is complete. Please review. What changed:

- **`notes/ring-buffer-design.md`** (new) — requirements + constraints for the ring buffer:
  - *Requirements:* no_std/no alloc over caller-provided memory; zero-copy in/out via zerocopy traits (`IntoBytes`/`FromBytes`/`KnownLayout`/`Immutable`); IPC-suitable `#[repr(C)]` layout with no internal pointers; SPSC lock-free first (MPMC not precluded); in-place reserve/commit + peek/release API; all `unsafe` encapsulated.
  - *Constraints:* power-of-two capacity (mask wrapping), free-running monotonic indices (no sacrificial slot), cache-line-separated head/tail, fixed-size `RingBuffer<T>` slots (variable-length deferred), zerocopy as the only dependency.
  - *Open questions* for the `-2` layout step: `AtomicU32` vs `AtomicUsize` (cross-process 32/64-bit argues fixed-width), batch vs single-slot release, and safe construction over an mmap'd region.
- **Bookkeeping:** ladder `-1` marked `(current)`, version bumped to `0.3.0-1`, `Commits:` backfilled in chores-01 for the two commits the last pushes made permanent (`[[1]]` template cleanup, `[[2]]` the `-0` pickup), and a design-notes link added to the In Progress block.

> DEVELOPER

In Constraints you say "Fixed-size slots" define slot, from my PoV a ring buffer holds M buffers of N size. M is power-of-two, N is modulo cache line size and appropriately aligned. Agreed?

> AGENT

Agreed, with one nuance worth writing down. Your model — M slots of N bytes, M a power of two, N a multiple of the cache line and cache-line aligned — is cleaner than what I wrote, because it decouples the slot geometry from the message type: the slot is a property of the buffer; `T` is a view into a slot, valid when `size_of::<T>() <= N` and `align_of::<T>()` divides the slot alignment. Cache-line-sized/aligned slots also kill producer/consumer false sharing on adjacent slots and give every `T` (align ≤ 64) a home for free.

The nuance: N being a cache-line multiple trades space for isolation — a 16-byte message burns 64 bytes. For an IPC-oriented design that's the right default; noting it as a deliberate trade keeps a future "packed slots" variant honest. Updating the doc:

  "new_string": "- **Slot geometry: M slots × N bytes** — a *slot* is one\n  fixed-size buffer in the ring; the ring holds M of them.\n  - M (capacity) is a power of two — index wrapping is a\n    mask, no division; checked at construction.\n  - N (slot size) is a multiple of the cache-line size, and\n    each slot is cache-line aligned — adjacent slots never\n    share a line, and any `T` with `align_of::<T>()` up to\n    the line size fits without padding logic.\n  - This trades space for isolation (a 16-byte message\n    occupies a full 64-byte slot) — the right default for\n    IPC; a packed-slot variant is a possible follow-on."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/ring-buffer-design.md",
  "old_string": "## Open questions\n\n- Atomic width for indices: `AtomicU32` vs `AtomicUsize` —\n  cross-process mapping between 32/64-bit peers argues for a\n  fixed-width `AtomicU32`. Decide in the API/layout step.\n- Whether the consumer releases slots one-at-a-time or in\n  batches (batch release amortizes the atomic store).\n- How construction over foreign memory (an mmap'd region) is\n  expressed safely — likely a `from_bytes`-style validated\n  cast via zerocopy's `KnownLayout`.",
  "new_string": "## Memory layout\n\nOne contiguous `#[repr(C)]` region, mappable into multiple\naddress spaces. Three parts, each cache-line aligned:\n\n```\noffset 0                 Header        (1 cache line)\noffset 64                producer line (1 cache line)\noffset 128               consumer line (1 cache line)\noffset 192               slots: M × N bytes\n```\n\n- **Header** (`#[repr(C, align(64))]`) — immutable after init:\n  - `magic: u32` — layout marker, rejects foreign regions.\n  - `layout_version: u32` — bumped on any layout change.\n  - `slot_size: u32` — N in bytes, cache-line multiple.\n  - `capacity: u32` — M, power of two; index mask is `M - 1`.\n- **Producer line** — `head: AtomicU32` + padding. Written\n  only by the producer, read by the consumer.\n- **Consumer line** — `tail: AtomicU32` + padding. Written\n  only by the consumer, read by the producer.\n- **Slots** — `M` slots of `N` bytes, each cache-line\n  aligned (N is a line multiple, so alignment follows from\n  the array base).\n\nIndex scheme (resolves the atomic-width question):\n\n- Indices are **fixed-width `AtomicU32`**, free-running\n  (never masked in storage) — fixed width keeps the layout\n  identical for 32- and 64-bit peers sharing the region.\n- Slot index = `counter & (M - 1)`; occupancy =\n  `head.wrapping_sub(tail)` — valid while `M <= 2^31`.\n- Empty: `head == tail`. Full: `head - tail == M`. No\n  sacrificial slot.\n\nOrdering: producer loads `tail` with `Acquire` and publishes\n`head` with `Release`; consumer mirrors (loads `head`\n`Acquire`, publishes `tail` `Release`).\n\n## API sketch\n\nConstruction (resolves the foreign-memory question):\n\n- `Ring::init(region: &mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:\n  u32) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` — writes the header, zeroes\n  indices; validates geometry (power-of-two M, line-multiple\n  N, region big enough, alignment).\n- `Ring::attach(region: &[u8]) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` —\n  validated cast of an already-initialized region (an mmap'd\n  segment): checks magic, layout_version, geometry against\n  the region length. Built on zerocopy's `KnownLayout`\n  validated reference casts — no `unsafe` at the call site.\n- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — the two\n  endpoint handles; each is `Send + !Sync` and exists at\n  most once per ring per process (SPSC discipline;\n  cross-process there is one producer process and one\n  consumer process by contract).\n\nProducer — reserve/commit, in place:\n\n- `producer.reserve::<T>() -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>`\n  — next free slot as `&mut T` (zerocopy `FromBytes +\n  IntoBytes + KnownLayout`); `T` geometry checked against\n  `slot_size` once.\n- `Reserved::commit(self)` — publishes the slot\n  (`head + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit abandons\n  the reservation (nothing published).\n\nConsumer — peek/release, in place:\n\n- `consumer.peek::<T>() -> Result<Peeked<'_, T>, Empty>` —\n  oldest unread slot as `&T`.\n- `Peeked::release(self)` — frees the slot (`tail + 1`,\n  `Release`). Dropping without release leaves the slot\n  unread — the next peek returns it again.\n\nBatch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —\nthe guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the\nsingle-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- Cross-process trust: `attach` validates the header, but a\n  hostile peer can scribble on the region afterward; the\n  consumer-side typed view means a torn/garbage message is\n  still *some* valid `T` (zerocopy guarantees no UB, not\n  semantic sense). Document as: the ring is safe against UB,\n  not against a malicious peer.\n- Whether `split()` needs a runtime once-guard in the header\n  (endpoint-claimed flags) or stays a documented contract."
}
```

> AGENT

Work for `0.3.0-2` is complete. Please review. What the design adds to `notes/ring-buffer-design.md`:

**Memory layout** — one contiguous `#[repr(C)]` region: a header line (magic, layout_version, slot_size N, capacity M), a producer-owned cache line (`head: AtomicU32`), a consumer-owned cache line (`tail: AtomicU32`), then M×N slots at offset 192. Indices are free-running fixed-width `AtomicU32` (resolves the atomic-width question — same layout for 32/64-bit peers); slot index is `counter & (M-1)`, empty is `head == tail`, full is `head - tail == M`, no sacrificial slot. Acquire/Release pairing on the opposing index.

**API sketch** —
- `Ring::init(region, slot_size, capacity)` and `Ring::attach(region)` (validated cast via zerocopy `KnownLayout` — resolves the foreign-memory question), then `ring.split()` → `Producer` + `Consumer` handles.
- Producer: `reserve::<T>()` → `&mut T` guard; `commit()` publishes, drop abandons.
- Consumer: `peek::<T>()` → `&T` guard; `release()` frees, drop leaves it unread.
- Batch release deferred (guard API leaves room for `release_n`).

**Open questions** (rolled forward): the cross-process trust model (safe against UB, not against a malicious peer scribbling post-attach), and whether `split()` needs endpoint-claimed flags in the header vs a documented contract.

Bookkeeping also done: ladder `-2` `(current)`, version `0.3.0-2`, chores `Commits:` backfilled with `[[3]]` (the `-1` SHA).

I suggest the following, Header is one struct with the following fields:

- `layout_version: u32`
- `slot_size: u32`
- `capacity: u32`
- `producer_idx: u32`
- `consumer_idx:: u32`

> AGENT

I'm on board with "one struct" — but I'd push back on the flat field list, on two grounds:

1. **False sharing.** With `producer_idx` and `consumer_idx` adjacent, every producer store invalidates the consumer's cached line and vice versa — the exact ping-pong the `-1` constraint ("cache-line separation") was written to avoid. On a shared-memory SPSC ring this is the dominant cost; it's why the current layout gives each index its own line.
2. **Atomicity.** The indices are concurrently written/read across threads/processes, so they must be `AtomicU32`, not plain `u32` — plain fields would be a data race (UB), and zerocopy-casting a shared region to a struct with non-atomic mutable fields doesn't make it sound.

The compromise that keeps your shape and the performance constraint: **one `Header` struct containing all six fields, with the two indices padded onto their own cache lines**:

```rust
#[repr(C, align(64))]
struct Header {
    // line 0 — immutable after init
    magic: u32,
    layout_version: u32,
    slot_size: u32,
    capacity: u32,
    _pad0: [u8; 48],
    // line 1 — producer-owned
    producer_idx: AtomicU32,
    _pad1: [u8; 60],
    // line 2 — consumer-owned
    consumer_idx: AtomicU32,
    _pad2: [u8; 60],
}
```

Same 192-byte prefix and offsets as the current design — it just names the whole thing one `Header` struct (which is genuinely better: one type owns the layout, `size_of::<Header>()` is the slot-array offset, and one zerocopy derive covers it). I'd also adopt your field names `producer_idx`/`consumer_idx` over `head`/`tail` — self-describing and unambiguous about who owns which.

Want me to update the design doc to this single-struct form (three-line `Header`, your names, indices atomic)?

> DEVELOPER

I don't think the math is correct for full, isn't it: `|producer_idx - consuder_idx| + 1 == M`

> AGENT

The doc's math is right for the scheme it uses — the confusion is which scheme the indices follow. Let me walk it:

In this design the indices are **free-running counters**, not positions in the array. `producer_idx` counts messages ever committed; `consumer_idx` counts messages ever released. Neither is masked in storage — masking happens only when converting to a slot position (`counter & (M-1)`). So the occupancy (messages written but not yet read) is exactly:

```
occupancy = producer_idx.wrapping_sub(consumer_idx)
```

Concrete example, M = 4: start `p = 0, c = 0` → occupancy 0, empty. Producer commits four messages without the consumer running: `p = 4, c = 0` → occupancy 4 = M, **full** — and correctly so, all four slots hold unread data. Consumer releases one: `c = 1` → occupancy 3, room for one more. So `producer_idx - consumer_idx == M` is the full test, `== 0` is empty, no `+1` anywhere.

The `|p − c| + 1 == M` shape you're recalling comes from the *other* classic scheme, where head and tail are already-masked array positions. There `p == c` is ambiguous (empty or full look identical), so you sacrifice one slot and declare full at `(p + 1) % M == c` — that's where the `+1` lives, and it wastes a slot. Free-running counters are exactly how this design avoids that (the "no sacrificial slot" bullet).

Two details that make it sound: no absolute value — unsigned `wrapping_sub` is correct even after `p` wraps past `u32::MAX` while `c` hasn't (modular arithmetic), whereas `|p − c|` would give a wrong huge value at the wrap boundary; and the `M <= 2^31` constraint keeps occupancy unambiguous in u32 space.

So I'd leave the doc as is — agreed?

> DEVELOPER

So the producer consumer indexes are full u32 values and masked only when used as an index not when incremented?

> AGENT

Exactly right. Stored and incremented as full free-running `u32` values — `commit` does `producer_idx.store(p + 1)` (well, wrapping add), never `% M`. The mask is applied only at the moment of slot access: `slot = &slots[(idx & (M - 1)) as usize]`.

Two properties make that work:

- **u32 overflow is harmless** — when `producer_idx` wraps past `u32::MAX` back to 0, `wrapping_sub` still gives correct occupancy, *because* M is a power of two: 2³² is a multiple of M, so the wrap doesn't shift the masked slot position either. (This is the quiet second reason for the power-of-two constraint, beyond cheap masking — with a non-power-of-two M, the u32 wrap would corrupt slot positions.)
- **The invariant `0 <= p − c <= M`** (in wrapping arithmetic) is maintained by each side checking before advancing: producer won't reserve when occupancy = M, consumer won't peek when it's 0.

That wrap subtlety is worth capturing — I'll add a line to the index-scheme bullets in the doc:

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "content": "//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided\n//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:\n//!\n//! - One `#[repr(C, align(64))]` [`Header`] spanning three cache\n//!   lines (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index),\n//!   followed by M slots of N bytes.\n//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot\n//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.\n//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a\n//!   [`Reserved`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a\n//!   [`Peeked`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no\n//!   serialization step.\n//!\n//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md\n\n#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]\n\nuse core::marker::PhantomData;\nuse core::mem::size_of;\nuse core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};\nuse core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};\nuse zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};\n\n/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.\n///\n/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region\n///   itself must be aligned to it.\npub const CACHE_LINE: usize = 64;\n\n/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign\n/// regions on attach.\nconst MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // \"ZCR1\"\n\n/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.\nconst LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 1;\n\n/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning\n/// three cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its\n/// own line.\n///\n/// - line 0: geometry, immutable after [`Ring::init`].\n/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.\n/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\npub struct Header {\n    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]).\n    magic: u32,\n    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).\n    layout_version: u32,\n    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    slot_size: u32,\n    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    capacity: u32,\n    _pad0: [u8; 48],\n    /// Free-running count of messages committed.\n    producer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad1: [u8; 60],\n    /// Free-running count of messages released.\n    consumer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad2: [u8; 60],\n}\n\nconst _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 3 * CACHE_LINE);\n\nimpl Header {\n    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).\n    fn mask(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.capacity - 1\n    }\n}\n\n/// Errors from [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`] region\n/// validation.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Error {\n    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned.\n    Misaligned,\n    /// Region is smaller than header + slots.\n    TooSmall,\n    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    BadSlotSize,\n    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.\n    BadCapacity,\n    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a ring region.\n    BadMagic,\n    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.\n    BadLayoutVersion,\n}\n\n/// `reserve` failed: every slot holds an uncommitted-or-unread\n/// message.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Full;\n\n/// `peek` failed: no unread messages.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Empty;\n\n/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two\n/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].\npub struct Ring<'a> {\n    /// The region's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Ring<'a> {\n    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.\n    ///\n    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE`]\n    ///   multiple.\n    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned and at least\n    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.\n    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        if region.as_ptr() as usize % CACHE_LINE != 0 {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if region.len() < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: region is exclusively borrowed for 'a, long\n        // enough (checked above), CACHE_LINE-aligned (checked\n        // above, and CACHE_LINE == align_of::<Header>()), and\n        // any byte pattern is a valid Header.\n        let header = unsafe { &mut *(region.as_mut_ptr() as *mut Header) };\n        header.magic = MAGIC;\n        header.layout_version = LAYOUT_VERSION;\n        header.slot_size = slot_size;\n        header.capacity = capacity;\n        header.producer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);\n        header.consumer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.as_mut_ptr().add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)\n    /// already initialized, validating its header.\n    ///\n    /// # Safety\n    ///\n    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive\n    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a\n    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).\n    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;\n    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).\n    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        if region as usize % CACHE_LINE != 0 {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if len < size_of::<Header>() {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: aligned + long enough (checked above); caller\n        // guarantees the memory is live and shared.\n        let header = unsafe { &*(region as *const Header) };\n        if header.magic != MAGIC {\n            return Err(Error::BadMagic);\n        }\n        if header.layout_version != LAYOUT_VERSION {\n            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);\n        }\n        validate_geometry(header.slot_size, header.capacity)?;\n        if len < region_size(header.slot_size, header.capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.\n    ///\n    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once\n    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and\n    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.\n    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {\n        (\n            Producer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n            Consumer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.\nfn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> usize {\n    size_of::<Header>() + slot_size as usize * capacity as usize\n}\n\n/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].\nfn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if slot_size == 0 || slot_size as usize % CACHE_LINE != 0 {\n        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);\n    }\n    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {\n        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per reserve/peek.\n///\n/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming\n///   error, not a runtime condition.\nfn check_type<T>(header: &Header) {\n    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= header.slot_size as usize, \"T larger than slot\");\n    assert!(\n        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE,\n        \"T alignment exceeds slot alignment\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.\n///\n/// - Returned range is `header.slot_size` bytes, cache-line\n///   aligned (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).\nfn slot_ptr(header: &Header, slots: *mut u8, idx: u32) -> *mut u8 {\n    let pos = (idx & header.mask()) as usize;\n    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot\n    // array validated at init/attach.\n    unsafe { slots.add(pos * header.slot_size as usize) }\n}\n\n/// The producing endpoint: `reserve` a slot, write in place,\n/// `commit`.\npub struct Producer<'a> {\n    /// The ring's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array.\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}\n\n// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it\n// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off\n// with Release/Acquire ordering.\nunsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}\n\nimpl<'a> Producer<'a> {\n    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, or [`Full`].\n    ///\n    /// - Dropping the guard without [`Reserved::commit`]\n    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).\n    pub fn reserve<T>(&mut self) -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.header);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        if p.wrapping_sub(c) == self.header.capacity {\n            return Err(Full);\n        }\n        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.header, self.slots, p);\n        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the producer exclusive\n        // access to this slot until commit; range validated at\n        // init/attach.\n        let bytes = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(bytes, self.header.slot_size as usize) };\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: check_type guarantees size, slot base guarantees\n        // alignment, so the prefix cast cannot fail.\n        let (msg, _) = T::mut_from_prefix(bytes).unwrap();\n        Ok(Reserved {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// A reserved slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then\n/// [`commit`](Reserved::commit).\npub struct Reserved<'p, T> {\n    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).\n    header: &'p Header,\n    /// The slot, viewed as the message type.\n    msg: &'p mut T,\n    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.\n    next_idx: u32,\n}\n\nimpl<T> Deref for Reserved<'_, T> {\n    type Target = T;\n    /// Read access to the in-slot message.\n    fn deref(&self) -> &T {\n        self.msg\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> DerefMut for Reserved<'_, T> {\n    /// Write access to the in-slot message.\n    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {\n        self.msg\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> Reserved<'_, T> {\n    /// Publish the slot to the consumer\n    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).\n    pub fn commit(self) {\n        self.header.producer_idx.store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);\n    }\n}\n\n/// The consuming endpoint: `peek` the oldest unread slot, read\n/// in place, `release`.\npub struct Consumer<'a> {\n    /// The ring's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array.\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}\n\n// SAFETY: the handle owns the consumer role; see the Producer\n// Send rationale.\nunsafe impl Send for Consumer<'_> {}\n\nimpl<'a> Consumer<'a> {\n    /// View the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, or [`Empty`].\n    ///\n    /// - Dropping the guard without [`Peeked::release`] leaves\n    ///   the slot unread — the next peek returns it again.\n    pub fn peek<T>(&mut self) -> Result<Peeked<'_, T>, Empty>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.header);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        if p == c {\n            return Err(Empty);\n        }\n        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.header, self.slots, c);\n        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the consumer exclusive\n        // access to this slot until release; range validated at\n        // init/attach.\n        let bytes = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(bytes as *const u8, self.header.slot_size as usize) };\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: check_type guarantees size, slot base guarantees\n        // alignment, so the prefix cast cannot fail.\n        let (msg, _) = T::ref_from_prefix(bytes).unwrap();\n        Ok(Peeked {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// A peeked slot: `Deref` to read the message, then\n/// [`release`](Peeked::release).\npub struct Peeked<'c, T> {\n    /// The ring's control block (for the release store).\n    header: &'c Header,\n    /// The slot, viewed as the message type.\n    msg: &'c T,\n    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.\n    next_idx: u32,\n}\n\nimpl<T> Deref for Peeked<'_, T> {\n    type Target = T;\n    /// Read access to the in-slot message.\n    fn deref(&self) -> &T {\n        self.msg\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> Peeked<'_, T> {\n    /// Free the slot for reuse (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`).\n    pub fn release(self) {\n        self.header.consumer_idx.store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be visible.\n    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]\n    #[repr(C)]\n    struct Msg {\n        seq: u64,\n        val: u64,\n    }\n\n    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store: header + 4 × 64B slots.\n    #[repr(C, align(64))]\n    struct Region([u8; 3 * CACHE_LINE + 4 * CACHE_LINE]);\n\n    impl Region {\n        fn new() -> Self {\n            Region([0; 3 * CACHE_LINE + 4 * CACHE_LINE])\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn init_rejects_bad_geometry() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).unwrap_err(), Error::BadSlotSize);\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).unwrap_err(), Error::BadSlotSize);\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).unwrap_err(), Error::BadCapacity);\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).unwrap_err(), Error::BadCapacity);\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).unwrap_err(), Error::TooSmall);\n        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).unwrap_err(), Error::Misaligned);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn attach_validates_header() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        // Not initialized yet: magic is zero.\n        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap_err();\n        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadMagic);\n        Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();\n        let ring = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(ring.header.slot_size, 64);\n        assert_eq!(ring.header.capacity, 4);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn roundtrip_full_empty() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        // Empty at start.\n        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());\n\n        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();\n            slot.seq = i;\n            slot.val = i * 10;\n            slot.commit();\n        }\n        assert!(prod.reserve::<Msg>().is_err());\n\n        // Drain in order; wrap: slot 0 is reused by seq 4.\n        for i in 0..4u64 {\n            let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();\n            assert_eq!(*msg, Msg { seq: i, val: i * 10 });\n            msg.release();\n        }\n        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());\n\n        // One more write lands in the masked-around slot 0.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 4;\n        slot.commit();\n        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);\n        msg.release();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn abandoned_guards_publish_nothing() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        // Reserve then drop: nothing published.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 99;\n        drop(slot);\n        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());\n\n        // Commit one, peek then drop: still unread, re-peekable.\n        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        slot.seq = 1;\n        slot.commit();\n        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);\n        drop(msg);\n        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);\n        msg.release();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn threaded_spsc() {\n        const COUNT: u64 = 100_000;\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\n        std::thread::scope(|s| {\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    loop {\n                        match prod.reserve::<Msg>() {\n                            Ok(mut slot) => {\n                                slot.seq = i;\n                                slot.val = i * 3;\n                                slot.commit();\n                                break;\n                            }\n                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            });\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    loop {\n                        match cons.peek::<Msg>() {\n                            Ok(msg) => {\n                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);\n                                msg.release();\n                                break;\n                            }\n                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            });\n        });\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Cargo cycle clean: fmt ✓, clippy `-D warnings` ✓, 5/5 tests pass (geometry validation, attach validation, roundtrip with wrap, guard abandonment, and a 100k-message threaded SPSC stress test).

Work for `0.3.0-3` is complete. Please review. What landed:

- **`Cargo.toml`** (new) — `zc-ring-x1` lib crate, edition 2021, sole dependency `zerocopy 0.8` (no default features, derive on). Carries the version-of-record `0.3.0-3` per versioning.md's Rust rule; **`version.toml` is deleted** — the flagged deviation, easy to revert.
- **`src/lib.rs`** (new, ~560 lines) — the design transcribed to code:
  - `Header` exactly as designed: `#[repr(C, align(64))]`, three cache lines, `AtomicU32` indices, with a compile-time `size_of == 192` assert.
  - `Ring::init` (safe, validates geometry/alignment/size) and `Ring::attach` (**unsafe fn** — cross-address-space attach can't be made safe in pure Rust semantics; the safety contract documents the MAP_SHARED-style requirement). `split()` consumes the ring into `Producer` + `Consumer`.
  - `reserve::<T>() → Reserved` (DerefMut, `commit()` publishes Release, drop abandons) and `peek::<T>() → Peeked` (Deref, `release()` frees, drop leaves unread). zerocopy `mut_from_prefix`/`ref_from_prefix` do the typed casts — no unsafe at the message boundary.
  - Free-running indices, mask at slot access, Acquire/Release pairing per the doc.
- **Bookkeeping** — ladder `-3` `(current)`, chores `Commits:` gained `[[4]]` (the `-2` SHA).

One design-doc divergence to note: the doc sketches `attach(region: &[u8])` as safe; the implementation made it `unsafe fn attach(*mut u8, len)`. I'd sync the doc at close-out. Ready for the commit description when you are.
