1	# Ring buffer design
2	
3	Design notes for the zero-copy ring buffer. This file uses
4	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It covers requirements,
5	constraints, the memory layout, and the API sketch; the
6	prototype crate validates it in the next cycle step.
7	
8	## Goal
9	
10	A ring buffer for passing typed messages between a producer and
11	a consumer with no copying at the boundary: the producer writes
12	its message directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads it
13	in place. The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and
14	inter-process (shared memory).
15	
16	## Requirements
17	
18	- **no_std** — the crate builds with `#![no_std]` and no
19	  `alloc`: fixed capacity, operating over a caller-provided
20	  memory region. Suitable for embedded and kernel-adjacent use.
21	- **Zero-copy via zerocopy** — messages are structs implementing
22	  the [zerocopy](https://docs.rs/zerocopy) traits
23	  (`IntoBytes`, `FromBytes`, `KnownLayout`, `Immutable`); the
24	  buffer transmutes slots into and out of `&T`/`&mut T` with no
25	  serialization step and no `unsafe` at the call site.
26	- **IPC-suitable layout** — the control block (the
27	  producer/consumer indices) and the slot array live in one
28	  contiguous region that
29	  can be mapped into multiple address spaces:
30	  - `#[repr(C)]` explicit layout, stable across processes
31	    compiled from the same source.
32	  - No pointers inside the region — offsets and indices only.
33	- **SPSC first** — single producer, single consumer, lock-free
34	  via atomic producer/consumer indices. MPMC is out of scope
35	  for this cycle;
36	  the layout should not preclude adding it later.
37	- **In-place access API** — reserve/commit on the producer side
38	  and peek/release on the consumer side, exposing references
39	  into the buffer rather than returning values by move.
40	- **Safety** — all `unsafe` encapsulated inside the crate with
41	  documented invariants; public API is safe.
42	
43	## Constraints
44	
45	- **Slot geometry: M slots × N bytes** — a *slot* is one
46	  fixed-size buffer in the ring; the ring holds M of them.
47	  - M (capacity) is a power of two — index wrapping is a
48	    mask, no division; checked at construction.
49	  - N (slot size) is a multiple of the cache-line size, and
50	    each slot is cache-line aligned — adjacent slots never
51	    share a line, and any `T` with `align_of::<T>()` up to
52	    the line size fits without padding logic.
53	  - This trades space for isolation (a 16-byte message
54	    occupies a full 64-byte slot) — the right default for
55	    IPC; a packed-slot variant is a possible follow-on.
56	- **Monotonic indices** — producer/consumer indices are
57	  free-running unsigned counters (wrap via masking), so
58	  full/empty are distinguishable without a separate flag or
59	  a sacrificial slot.
60	- **Cache-line separation** — producer- and consumer-owned
61	  indices are padded/aligned to separate cache lines to avoid
62	  false sharing.
63	- **Typed access over untyped slots** — the slot geometry
64	  (M, N) is a property of the buffer, independent of any
65	  message type; a `T` is a zerocopy view into a slot, valid
66	  when `size_of::<T>() <= N` and `align_of::<T>()` divides
67	  the slot alignment (checked at construction or compile
68	  time). Variable-length framed messages are a possible
69	  follow-on, not in this cycle.
70	- **zerocopy, core-only** — depend on zerocopy with default
71	  features compatible with `no_std`; no other runtime
72	  dependencies.
73	
74	## Memory layout
75	
76	One contiguous `#[repr(C)]` region, mappable into multiple
77	address spaces: a `Header` struct followed by the slot array.
78	The `Header` is one struct spanning three cache lines, so each
79	concurrently-written index owns its own line:
80	
81	```rust
82	#[repr(C, align(64))]
83	struct Header {
84	    // line 0 — immutable after init
85	    magic: u32,          // layout marker, rejects foreign regions
86	    layout_version: u32, // bumped on any layout change
87	    slot_size: u32,      // N in bytes, cache-line multiple
88	    capacity: u32,       // M, power of two; index mask is M - 1
89	    _pad0: [u8; 48],
90	    // line 1 — producer-owned
91	    producer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by producer only
92	    _pad1: [u8; 60],
93	    // line 2 — consumer-owned
94	    consumer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by consumer only
95	    _pad2: [u8; 60],
96	}
97	```
98	
99	```
100	offset 0                    Header (192 bytes, 3 cache lines)
101	offset size_of::<Header>()  slots: M × N bytes
102	```
103	
104	- One type owns the whole control-block layout:
105	  `size_of::<Header>()` is the slot-array offset, and a
106	  single zerocopy derive covers validation.
107	- **Slots** — `M` slots of `N` bytes, each cache-line
108	  aligned (N is a line multiple, so alignment follows from
109	  the array base).
110	
111	Index scheme (resolves the atomic-width question):
112	
113	- **Fixed-width `AtomicU32`, free-running**:
114	  - increments are wrapping adds, never masked in storage
115	  - fixed width keeps the layout identical for 32- and
116	    64-bit peers sharing the region
117	- **Masked only at slot access**:
118	  - slot position = `idx & (M - 1)`
119	- **Occupancy** = `producer_idx.wrapping_sub(consumer_idx)`
120	  (valid while `M <= 2^31`):
121	  - empty: `producer_idx == consumer_idx`
122	  - full: `producer_idx - consumer_idx == M`
123	  - no sacrificial slot
124	- **u32 wrap at `2^32` is harmless** because M is a power
125	  of two:
126	  - `2^32 % M == 0`, so occupancy math and masked slot
127	    positions both carry across the wrap unchanged
128	- **Example** (M = 4, `p` = producer_idx, `c` = consumer_idx):
129	  - start: `p = 0, c = 0` — occupancy 0, empty
130	  - producer commits 4 messages: `p = 4, c = 0` — occupancy
131	    4 == M, full; slot positions written were 0, 1, 2, 3
132	  - consumer releases 1: `p = 4, c = 1` — occupancy 3, one
133	    slot free; the next reserve writes slot `4 & 3 == 0`
134	
135	Ordering: the producer loads `consumer_idx` with `Acquire`
136	and publishes `producer_idx` with `Release`; the consumer
137	mirrors (loads `producer_idx` `Acquire`, publishes
138	`consumer_idx` `Release`).
139	
140	## API sketch
141	
142	Construction (resolves the foreign-memory question):
143	
144	- `Ring::init(region: &mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:
145	  u32) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` — writes the header, zeroes
146	  indices; validates geometry (power-of-two M, line-multiple
147	  N, region big enough, alignment).
148	- `Ring::attach(region: &[u8]) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` —
149	  validated cast of an already-initialized region (an mmap'd
150	  segment): checks magic, layout_version, geometry against
151	  the region length. Built on zerocopy's `KnownLayout`
152	  validated reference casts — no `unsafe` at the call site.
153	- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — the two
154	  endpoint handles; each is `Send + !Sync` and exists at
155	  most once per ring per process (SPSC discipline;
156	  cross-process there is one producer process and one
157	  consumer process by contract).
158	
159	Producer — reserve/commit, in place:
160	
161	- `producer.reserve::<T>() -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>`
162	  — next free slot as `&mut T` (zerocopy `FromBytes +
163	  IntoBytes + KnownLayout`); `T` geometry checked against
164	  `slot_size` once.
165	- `Reserved::commit(self)` — publishes the slot
166	  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit
167	  abandons the reservation (nothing published).
168	
169	Consumer — peek/release, in place:
170	
171	- `consumer.peek::<T>() -> Result<Peeked<'_, T>, Empty>` —
172	  oldest unread slot as `&T`.
173	- `Peeked::release(self)` — frees the slot
174	  (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without release
175	  leaves the slot unread — the next peek returns it again.
176	
177	Batch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —
178	the guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the
179	single-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.
180	
181	## Open questions
182	
183	- Cross-process trust: `attach` validates the header, but a
184	  hostile peer can scribble on the region afterward; the
185	  consumer-side typed view means a torn/garbage message is
186	  still *some* valid `T` (zerocopy guarantees no UB, not
187	  semantic sense). Document as: the ring is safe against UB,
188	  not against a malicious peer.
189	- Whether `split()` needs a runtime once-guard in the header
190	  (endpoint-claimed flags) or stays a documented contract.
191	
```

```
1	//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
2	//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
3	//!
4	//! - One `#[repr(C, align(64))]` [`Header`] spanning three cache
5	//!   lines (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index),
6	//!   followed by M slots of N bytes.
7	//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
8	//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
9	//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
10	//!   [`Reserved`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
11	//!   [`Peeked`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
12	//!   serialization step.
13	//!
14	//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md
15	
16	#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]
17	
18	use core::marker::PhantomData;
19	use core::mem::size_of;
20	use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
21	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
22	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
23	
24	/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.
25	///
26	/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region
27	///   itself must be aligned to it.
28	pub const CACHE_LINE: usize = 64;
29	
30	/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign
31	/// regions on attach.
32	const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // "ZCR1"
33	
34	/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.
35	const LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 1;
36	
37	/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning
38	/// three cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its
39	/// own line.
40	///
41	/// - line 0: geometry, immutable after [`Ring::init`].
42	/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.
43	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.
44	#[repr(C, align(64))]
45	pub struct Header {
46	    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]).
47	    magic: u32,
48	    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
49	    layout_version: u32,
50	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.
51	    slot_size: u32,
52	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.
53	    capacity: u32,
54	    _pad0: [u8; 48],
55	    /// Free-running count of messages committed.
56	    producer_idx: AtomicU32,
57	    _pad1: [u8; 60],
58	    /// Free-running count of messages released.
59	    consumer_idx: AtomicU32,
60	    _pad2: [u8; 60],
61	}
62	
63	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 3 * CACHE_LINE);
64	
65	impl Header {
66	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
67	    fn mask(&self) -> u32 {
68	        self.capacity - 1
69	    }
70	}
71	
72	/// Errors from [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`] region
73	/// validation.
74	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
75	pub enum Error {
76	    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned.
77	    Misaligned,
78	    /// Region is smaller than header + slots.
79	    TooSmall,
80	    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.
81	    BadSlotSize,
82	    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.
83	    BadCapacity,
84	    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a ring region.
85	    BadMagic,
86	    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.
87	    BadLayoutVersion,
88	}
89	
90	/// `reserve` failed: every slot holds an uncommitted-or-unread
91	/// message.
92	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
93	pub struct Full;
94	
95	/// `peek` failed: no unread messages.
96	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
97	pub struct Empty;
98	
99	/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two
100	/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].
101	pub struct Ring<'a> {
102	    /// The region's control block.
103	    header: &'a Header,
104	    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).
105	    slots: *mut u8,
106	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
107	}
108	
109	impl<'a> Ring<'a> {
110	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
111	    ///
112	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE`]
113	    ///   multiple.
114	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.
115	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned and at least
116	    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.
117	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
118	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
119	        if !(region.as_ptr() as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {
120	            return Err(Error::Misaligned);
121	        }
122	        if region.len() < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
123	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
124	        }
125	        // SAFETY: region is exclusively borrowed for 'a, long
126	        // enough (checked above), CACHE_LINE-aligned (checked
127	        // above, and CACHE_LINE == align_of::<Header>()), and
128	        // any byte pattern is a valid Header.
129	        let header = unsafe { &mut *(region.as_mut_ptr() as *mut Header) };
130	        header.magic = MAGIC;
131	        header.layout_version = LAYOUT_VERSION;
132	        header.slot_size = slot_size;
133	        header.capacity = capacity;
134	        header.producer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);
135	        header.consumer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);
136	        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.
137	        let slots = unsafe { region.as_mut_ptr().add(size_of::<Header>()) };
138	        Ok(Ring {
139	            header,
140	            slots,
141	            _region: PhantomData,
142	        })
143	    }
144	
145	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
146	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
147	    ///
148	    /// # Safety
149	    ///
150	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
151	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
152	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
153	    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;
154	    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).
155	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
156	        if !(region as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {
157	            return Err(Error::Misaligned);
158	        }
159	        if len < size_of::<Header>() {
160	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
161	        }
162	        // SAFETY: aligned + long enough (checked above); caller
163	        // guarantees the memory is live and shared.
164	        let header = unsafe { &*(region as *const Header) };
165	        if header.magic != MAGIC {
166	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
167	        }
168	        if header.layout_version != LAYOUT_VERSION {
169	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
170	        }
171	        validate_geometry(header.slot_size, header.capacity)?;
172	        if len < region_size(header.slot_size, header.capacity) {
173	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
174	        }
175	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.
176	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
177	        Ok(Ring {
178	            header,
179	            slots,
180	            _region: PhantomData,
181	        })
182	    }
183	
184	    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.
185	    ///
186	    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once
187	    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and
188	    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.
189	    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {
190	        (
191	            Producer {
192	                header: self.header,
193	                slots: self.slots,
194	                _region: PhantomData,
195	            },
196	            Consumer {
197	                header: self.header,
198	                slots: self.slots,
199	                _region: PhantomData,
200	            },
201	        )
202	    }
203	}
204	
205	/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.
206	fn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> usize {
207	    size_of::<Header>() + slot_size as usize * capacity as usize
208	}
209	
210	/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
211	fn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
212	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {
213	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
214	    }
215	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {
216	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
217	    }
218	    Ok(())
219	}
220	
221	/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per reserve/peek.
222	///
223	/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming
224	///   error, not a runtime condition.
225	fn check_type<T>(header: &Header) {
226	    assert!(
227	        size_of::<T>() <= header.slot_size as usize,
228	        "T larger than slot"
229	    );
230	    assert!(
231	        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE,
232	        "T alignment exceeds slot alignment"
233	    );
234	}
235	
236	/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.
237	///
238	/// - Returned range is `header.slot_size` bytes, cache-line
239	///   aligned (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).
240	fn slot_ptr(header: &Header, slots: *mut u8, idx: u32) -> *mut u8 {
241	    let pos = (idx & header.mask()) as usize;
242	    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot
243	    // array validated at init/attach.
244	    unsafe { slots.add(pos * header.slot_size as usize) }
245	}
246	
247	/// The producing endpoint: `reserve` a slot, write in place,
248	/// `commit`.
249	pub struct Producer<'a> {
250	    /// The ring's control block.
251	    header: &'a Header,
252	    /// Base of the slot array.
253	    slots: *mut u8,
254	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
255	}
256	
257	// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it
258	// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off
259	// with Release/Acquire ordering.
260	unsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}
261	
262	impl<'a> Producer<'a> {
263	    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, or [`Full`].
264	    ///
265	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`Reserved::commit`]
266	    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).
267	    pub fn reserve<T>(&mut self) -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>
268	    where
269	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
270	    {
271	        check_type::<T>(self.header);
272	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
273	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
274	        if p.wrapping_sub(c) == self.header.capacity {
275	            return Err(Full);
276	        }
277	        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.header, self.slots, p);
278	        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the producer exclusive
279	        // access to this slot until commit; range validated at
280	        // init/attach.
281	        let bytes =
282	            unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(bytes, self.header.slot_size as usize) };
283	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
284	        // OK: check_type guarantees size, slot base guarantees
285	        // alignment, so the prefix cast cannot fail.
286	        let (msg, _) = T::mut_from_prefix(bytes).unwrap();
287	        Ok(Reserved {
288	            header: self.header,
289	            msg,
290	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
291	        })
292	    }
293	}
294	
295	/// A reserved slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then
296	/// [`commit`](Reserved::commit).
297	pub struct Reserved<'p, T> {
298	    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).
299	    header: &'p Header,
300	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type.
301	    msg: &'p mut T,
302	    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.
303	    next_idx: u32,
304	}
305	
306	impl<T> Deref for Reserved<'_, T> {
307	    type Target = T;
308	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
309	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
310	        self.msg
311	    }
312	}
313	
314	impl<T> DerefMut for Reserved<'_, T> {
315	    /// Write access to the in-slot message.
316	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
317	        self.msg
318	    }
319	}
320	
321	impl<T> Reserved<'_, T> {
322	    /// Publish the slot to the consumer
323	    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
324	    pub fn commit(self) {
325	        self.header
326	            .producer_idx
327	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
328	    }
329	}
330	
331	/// The consuming endpoint: `peek` the oldest unread slot, read
332	/// in place, `release`.
333	pub struct Consumer<'a> {
334	    /// The ring's control block.
335	    header: &'a Header,
336	    /// Base of the slot array.
337	    slots: *mut u8,
338	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
339	}
340	
341	// SAFETY: the handle owns the consumer role; see the Producer
342	// Send rationale.
343	unsafe impl Send for Consumer<'_> {}
344	
345	impl<'a> Consumer<'a> {
346	    /// View the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, or [`Empty`].
347	    ///
348	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`Peeked::release`] leaves
349	    ///   the slot unread — the next peek returns it again.
350	    pub fn peek<T>(&mut self) -> Result<Peeked<'_, T>, Empty>
351	    where
352	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
353	    {
354	        check_type::<T>(self.header);
355	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
356	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
357	        if p == c {
358	            return Err(Empty);
359	        }
360	        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.header, self.slots, c);
361	        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the consumer exclusive
362	        // access to this slot until release; range validated at
363	        // init/attach.
364	        let bytes = unsafe {
365	            core::slice::from_raw_parts(bytes as *const u8, self.header.slot_size as usize)
366	        };
367	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
368	        // OK: check_type guarantees size, slot base guarantees
369	        // alignment, so the prefix cast cannot fail.
370	        let (msg, _) = T::ref_from_prefix(bytes).unwrap();
371	        Ok(Peeked {
372	            header: self.header,
373	            msg,
374	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
375	        })
376	    }
377	}
378	
379	/// A peeked slot: `Deref` to read the message, then
380	/// [`release`](Peeked::release).
381	pub struct Peeked<'c, T> {
382	    /// The ring's control block (for the release store).
383	    header: &'c Header,
384	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type.
385	    msg: &'c T,
386	    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.
387	    next_idx: u32,
388	}
389	
390	impl<T> Deref for Peeked<'_, T> {
391	    type Target = T;
392	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
393	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
394	        self.msg
395	    }
396	}
397	
398	impl<T> Peeked<'_, T> {
399	    /// Free the slot for reuse (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
400	    pub fn release(self) {
401	        self.header
402	            .consumer_idx
403	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
404	    }
405	}
406	
407	#[cfg(test)]
408	mod tests {
409	    use super::*;
410	
411	    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be visible.
412	    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
413	    #[repr(C)]
414	    struct Msg {
415	        seq: u64,
416	        val: u64,
417	    }
418	
419	    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store: header + 4 × 64B slots.
420	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
421	    struct Region([u8; 3 * CACHE_LINE + 4 * CACHE_LINE]);
422	
423	    impl Region {
424	        fn new() -> Self {
425	            Region([0; 3 * CACHE_LINE + 4 * CACHE_LINE])
426	        }
427	    }
428	
429	    #[test]
430	    fn init_rejects_bad_geometry() {
431	        let mut r = Region::new();
432	        assert_eq!(
433	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),
434	            Error::BadSlotSize
435	        );
436	        assert_eq!(
437	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),
438	            Error::BadSlotSize
439	        );
440	        assert_eq!(
441	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),
442	            Error::BadCapacity
443	        );
444	        assert_eq!(
445	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),
446	            Error::BadCapacity
447	        );
448	        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(), Error::TooSmall);
449	        assert_eq!(
450	            Ring::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
451	            Error::Misaligned
452	        );
453	    }
454	
455	    #[test]
456	    fn attach_validates_header() {
457	        let mut r = Region::new();
458	        // Not initialized yet: magic is zero.
459	        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }
460	            .err()
461	            .unwrap();
462	        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadMagic);
463	        Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
464	        let ring = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap();
465	        assert_eq!(ring.header.slot_size, 64);
466	        assert_eq!(ring.header.capacity, 4);
467	    }
468	
469	    #[test]
470	    fn roundtrip_full_empty() {
471	        let mut r = Region::new();
472	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
473	
474	        // Empty at start.
475	        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());
476	
477	        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full.
478	        for i in 0..4u64 {
479	            let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();
480	            slot.seq = i;
481	            slot.val = i * 10;
482	            slot.commit();
483	        }
484	        assert!(prod.reserve::<Msg>().is_err());
485	
486	        // Drain in order; wrap: slot 0 is reused by seq 4.
487	        for i in 0..4u64 {
488	            let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();
489	            assert_eq!(
490	                *msg,
491	                Msg {
492	                    seq: i,
493	                    val: i * 10
494	                }
495	            );
496	            msg.release();
497	        }
498	        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());
499	
500	        // One more write lands in the masked-around slot 0.
501	        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();
502	        slot.seq = 4;
503	        slot.commit();
504	        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();
505	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);
506	        msg.release();
507	    }
508	
509	    #[test]
510	    // Dropping the guards is the behavior under test; they have
511	    // no Drop impl by design (abandon = do nothing).
512	    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]
513	    fn abandoned_guards_publish_nothing() {
514	        let mut r = Region::new();
515	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
516	
517	        // Reserve then drop: nothing published.
518	        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();
519	        slot.seq = 99;
520	        drop(slot);
521	        assert!(cons.peek::<Msg>().is_err());
522	
523	        // Commit one, peek then drop: still unread, re-peekable.
524	        let mut slot = prod.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();
525	        slot.seq = 1;
526	        slot.commit();
527	        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();
528	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
529	        drop(msg);
530	        let msg = cons.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();
531	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
532	        msg.release();
533	    }
534	
535	    #[test]
536	    fn threaded_spsc() {
537	        const COUNT: u64 = 100_000;
538	        let mut r = Region::new();
539	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
540	
541	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
542	            s.spawn(move || {
543	                for i in 0..COUNT {
544	                    loop {
545	                        match prod.reserve::<Msg>() {
546	                            Ok(mut slot) => {
547	                                slot.seq = i;
548	                                slot.val = i * 3;
549	                                slot.commit();
550	                                break;
551	                            }
552	                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
553	                        }
554	                    }
555	                }
556	            });
557	            s.spawn(move || {
558	                for i in 0..COUNT {
559	                    loop {
560	                        match cons.peek::<Msg>() {
561	                            Ok(msg) => {
562	                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
563	                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
564	                                msg.release();
565	                                break;
566	                            }
567	                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
568	                        }
569	                    }
570	                }
571	            });
572	        });
573	    }
574	}
575

> AGENT

Miri confirms the aliasing bug on the spot. Full review below.

---

## Template sync (quick)

The diffs are tiny and exactly what you said: the template absorbed our distinct-per-step-titles convention — AGENTS.md keeps the full paragraph but swaps our project-specific example (`ring buffer`) for a generic `config loader`, and cycle-protocol.md collapses the bullet we added into a one-line pointer at AGENTS.md, removing the restatement (which was indeed a duplication we introduced). Adopting is a verbatim copy of both files; I'd do it as a small standalone commit so these generic files stay byte-identical to the template. Awaiting your go.

## Design review

Overall: the concurrency design is sound — the index scheme, full/empty math, and Acquire/Release protocol are textbook-correct SPSC, and the guard API cleanly encodes slot ownership in the borrow checker. I found two real implementation bugs (one confirmed by Miri just now), one design-level gap in the IPC story, and a set of doc/impl divergences to sync at close-out.

### 1. Confirmed UB: Stacked Borrows violation in `Ring::init` (src/lib.rs:137)

Miri flags exactly this: line 129 creates `&mut Header` through `region.as_mut_ptr()`, then line 137 calls `region.as_mut_ptr()` *again* — a fresh unique retag of the whole slice that invalidates the `header` reference we're about to store in the `Ring`. Every later use of `self.header` is UB under Stacked Borrows. It's benign on today's compilers but it's exactly the class of thing Miri exists to catch, and the fix is a two-line change: call `as_mut_ptr()` once, derive both `header` and `slots` from that single base pointer. (`attach` is clean — raw-pointer arithmetic doesn't retag.)

### 2. Soundness hole on 32-bit: `region_size` overflow (src/lib.rs:206)

`size_of::<Header>() + slot_size as usize * capacity as usize` — on a 32-bit target, `slot_size = 2^26` × `capacity = 2^6` wraps to 0, so `region_size` returns 192 and a 192-byte region passes validation; the first `reserve` then writes far out of bounds **through the safe API**. The design explicitly targets 32-bit peers (it's the argument for `AtomicU32`), so this is in scope. Same hole in `attach`, where a corrupt/hostile header could pass the length check. On 64-bit it can't overflow (max ~2^63). Fix: compute in `u64` or use `checked_mul`/`checked_add`.

### 3. Design gap: the attach handshake

Three related problems, one fix:

- **`init` writes `magic` first**, geometry after. A peer attaching concurrently can see valid magic + garbage geometry. Magic should be the *last* thing written, with Release ordering, and attach should load it with Acquire.
- **Geometry fields are plain `u32`** read through a shared mapping. If a peer (even a buggy cooperating one) writes them while we read, that's a data race — UB — which quietly contradicts the doc's trust claim ("safe against UB, not against a malicious peer"). Making them `AtomicU32` (identical layout, Relaxed reads) makes the claim actually true.
- **Per-op re-reads of peer-writable fields**: `reserve`/`peek` re-read `slot_size`/`capacity` through the shared header on every call. Snapshotting geometry into `Producer`/`Consumer` at construction shrinks the trust surface to attach time and drops a load from the hot path.

The `split()` once-guard open question could fold into the same header line (an endpoint-claimed flags word) in a future layout_version.

### 4. Doc/impl divergences (sync at close-out)

- Doc: safe `attach(region: &[u8])`, "no unsafe at the call site". Impl: `unsafe fn attach(*mut u8, len)` — the impl is right, and the doc should record *why*: zerocopy's shared-ref validated casts require `Immutable`, which `Header` correctly isn't (atomics), and cross-address-space attach is inherently a caller-vouched contract. The "no unsafe" requirement should be scoped to the message path, where it does hold.
- Doc: "`T` geometry checked against `slot_size` once". Impl: panicking assert on every `reserve`/`peek`. Fine for a prototype; typed endpoints (`Producer<T>` checking at split) would honor the doc and drop the per-call cost — worth an entry as API evolution.

### 5. Observations (record, don't change this cycle)

- `CACHE_LINE = 64` plays two roles: a frozen layout-ABI constant (correct — it's baked into `layout_version 1`) and a false-sharing pad (on aarch64 the effective pitch is 128B; crossbeam pads to 128). Worth one doc sentence separating the roles.
- The `capacity > 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are ≤ 2^31) — harmless intent documentation; a comment would stop a future reader from puzzling over it.
- Waiting is spin-only by design; there's no wakeup story (futex/eventfd) — fine for this scope, worth an Ideas entry.
- Minor polish: `Error` could impl `Display`/`core::error::Error`; an `occupancy()`/`is_empty()` accessor pair is cheap; `reserve`'s full check could use `>=` as free hostile-header hardening.
- Test gaps: nothing exercises indices near the u32 wrap (needs a test-only index setter — the power-of-two argument deserves a proof-by-test), and Miri just earned a permanent spot in the validation ladder (non-threaded tests; the threaded one would crawl). Loom is the heavier follow-on for ordering exploration.

### What's verified solid

Commit's Release store pairs with peek's Acquire load (slot writes happen-before consumer reads); release's Release pairs with reserve's Acquire (consumer reads happen-before slot reuse) — the anti-dependency direction people usually get wrong. Own-index Relaxed loads are single-writer-correct. Guard exclusivity via `&mut` prevents double-reserve statically; abandon semantics are clean and tested; the zerocopy prefix-cast unwraps are genuinely infallible given `check_type` plus slot alignment. Tests pass 5/5 including the 100k threaded stress.

---

**Suggested path:** a `-4` fix commit (items 1–3: single-base-pointer init, u64 sizing, magic-last + atomic geometry + snapshot) with Miri added to the ladder's validation step, then doc-sync + the observations into the design doc / todo, then close-out. Template sync as its own small commit whenever you like. One note: I installed the Miri component on your existing nightly toolchain to run the verification. How do you want to proceed?

I'd do the same: **land the template sync as a standalone commit right after `0.3.0` close-out.** (The workable alternative is folding it into `0.4.0-0` Preparation, since the review's findings will seed a next cycle anyway — but standalone keeps the generic files in sync sooner.)
- Meanwhile the review produced real doc-sync work for the *design doc* (attach unsafety rationale, checked-once wording, CACHE_LINE roles, refreshed open questions). That deserves its own commit rather than bloating close-out, which the protocol wants bookkeeping-only. So: **`-5 docs: sync ring buffer design doc to as-built`**, then close-out.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning\n/// three cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its\n/// own line.\n///\n/// - line 0: geometry, immutable after [`Ring::init`].\n/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.\n/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\npub struct Header {\n    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]).\n    magic: u32,\n    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).\n    layout_version: u32,\n    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    slot_size: u32,\n    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    capacity: u32,\n    _pad0: [u8; 48],\n    /// Free-running count of messages committed.\n    producer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad1: [u8; 60],\n    /// Free-running count of messages released.\n    consumer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad2: [u8; 60],\n}\n\nconst _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 3 * CACHE_LINE);\n\nimpl Header {\n    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).\n    fn mask(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.capacity - 1\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning\n/// three cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its\n/// own line.\n///\n/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Ring::init`] with `magic`\n///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter.\n/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.\n/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.\n/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a peer\n///   at any time, so even \"immutable\" fields must be free of\n///   data races. `AtomicU32` has the same layout as `u32`, so\n///   this does not affect `layout_version`.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\npub struct Header {\n    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]); stored last by init\n    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`), so a\n    /// peer that observes it also observes the geometry.\n    magic: AtomicU32,\n    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).\n    layout_version: AtomicU32,\n    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    slot_size: AtomicU32,\n    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.\n    capacity: AtomicU32,\n    _pad0: [u8; 48],\n    /// Free-running count of messages committed.\n    producer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad1: [u8; 60],\n    /// Free-running count of messages released.\n    consumer_idx: AtomicU32,\n    _pad2: [u8; 60],\n}\n\nconst _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 3 * CACHE_LINE);"
}
```

  "old_string": "/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two\n/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].\npub struct Ring<'a> {\n    /// The region's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two\n/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].\n///\n/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at\n///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer\n///   could scribble on, so a hostile peer can degrade the ring\n///   to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, never to UB.\npub struct Ring<'a> {\n    /// The region's control block.\n    header: &'a Header,\n    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).\n    slots: *mut u8,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.\n    slot_size: u32,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.\n    capacity: u32,\n    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).\n    mask: u32,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        if !(region.as_ptr() as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if region.len() < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: region is exclusively borrowed for 'a, long\n        // enough (checked above), CACHE_LINE-aligned (checked\n        // above, and CACHE_LINE == align_of::<Header>()), and\n        // any byte pattern is a valid Header.\n        let header = unsafe { &mut *(region.as_mut_ptr() as *mut Header) };\n        header.magic = MAGIC;\n        header.layout_version = LAYOUT_VERSION;\n        header.slot_size = slot_size;\n        header.capacity = capacity;\n        header.producer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);\n        header.consumer_idx = AtomicU32::new(0);\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.as_mut_ptr().add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }",
  "new_string": "    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        if !(region.as_ptr() as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if (region.len() as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // Taken exactly once: a second `as_mut_ptr()` would\n        // retag the slice and invalidate `header` under Stacked\n        // Borrows (Miri-verified).\n        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();\n        // SAFETY: region is exclusively borrowed for 'a, long\n        // enough (checked above), CACHE_LINE-aligned (checked\n        // above, and CACHE_LINE == align_of::<Header>()), and\n        // any byte pattern is a valid Header (all-atomic\n        // fields, plain-byte padding).\n        let header = unsafe { &*(base as *const Header) };\n        header.layout_version.store(LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must\n        // never observe MAGIC before the geometry it validates.\n        // (Re-initializing a region a peer is already attached\n        // to is outside the contract.)\n        header.magic.store(MAGIC, Ordering::Release);\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            slot_size,\n            capacity,\n            mask: capacity - 1,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        if !(region as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if len < size_of::<Header>() {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: aligned + long enough (checked above); caller\n        // guarantees the memory is live and shared.\n        let header = unsafe { &*(region as *const Header) };\n        if header.magic != MAGIC {\n            return Err(Error::BadMagic);\n        }\n        if header.layout_version != LAYOUT_VERSION {\n            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);\n        }\n        validate_geometry(header.slot_size, header.capacity)?;\n        if len < region_size(header.slot_size, header.capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }",
  "new_string": "    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        if !(region as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n            return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n        }\n        if len < size_of::<Header>() {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: aligned + long enough (checked above); caller\n        // guarantees the memory is live and shared.\n        let header = unsafe { &*(region as *const Header) };\n        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a\n        // valid magic means the geometry stores are visible.\n        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MAGIC {\n            return Err(Error::BadMagic);\n        }\n        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != LAYOUT_VERSION {\n            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);\n        }\n        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.\n        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;\n        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.\n        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };\n        Ok(Ring {\n            header,\n            slots,\n            slot_size,\n            capacity,\n            mask: capacity - 1,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {\n        (\n            Producer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n            Consumer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.\nfn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> usize {\n    size_of::<Header>() + slot_size as usize * capacity as usize\n}\n\n/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].\nfn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);\n    }\n    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {\n        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per reserve/peek.\n///\n/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming\n///   error, not a runtime condition.\nfn check_type<T>(header: &Header) {\n    assert!(\n        size_of::<T>() <= header.slot_size as usize,\n        \"T larger than slot\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE,\n        \"T alignment exceeds slot alignment\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.\n///\n/// - Returned range is `header.slot_size` bytes, cache-line\n///   aligned (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).\nfn slot_ptr(header: &Header, slots: *mut u8, idx: u32) -> *mut u8 {\n    let pos = (idx & header.mask()) as usize;\n    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot\n    // array validated at init/attach.\n    unsafe { slots.add(pos * header.slot_size as usize) }\n}",
  "new_string": "    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {\n        (\n            Producer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                slot_size: self.slot_size,\n                capacity: self.capacity,\n                mask: self.mask,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n            Consumer {\n                header: self.header,\n                slots: self.slots,\n                slot_size: self.slot_size,\n                mask: self.mask,\n                _region: PhantomData,\n            },\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.\n///\n/// - Computed in u64: the usize product can wrap on 32-bit\n///   targets (e.g. N = 2^26, M = 2^6), which would validate a\n///   tiny region and let safe code write out of bounds.\nfn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {\n    size_of::<Header>() as u64 + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64\n}\n\n/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].\nfn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);\n    }\n    // The `> 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are\n    // <= 2^31); it stays to document the M bound the occupancy\n    // math relies on.\n    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {\n        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per reserve/peek.\n///\n/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming\n///   error, not a runtime condition.\nfn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {\n    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize, \"T larger than slot\");\n    assert!(\n        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE,\n        \"T alignment exceeds slot alignment\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.\n///\n/// - Returned range is `slot_size` bytes, cache-line aligned\n///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).\nfn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {\n    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;\n    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot\n    // array validated at init/attach.\n    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "        check_type::<T>(self.header);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        if p.wrapping_sub(c) == self.header.capacity {\n            return Err(Full);\n        }\n        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.header, self.slots, p);\n        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the producer exclusive\n        // access to this slot until commit; range validated at\n        // init/attach.\n        let bytes =\n            unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(bytes, self.header.slot_size as usize) };",
  "new_string": "        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        // `>=`, not `==`: a peer-corrupted consumer_idx makes\n        // occupancy look huge — fail Full, never hand out a\n        // slot the protocol doesn't own.\n        if p.wrapping_sub(c) >= self.capacity {\n            return Err(Full);\n        }\n        let bytes = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size);\n        // SAFETY: index protocol gives the producer exclusive\n        // access to this slot until commit; range validated at\n        // init/attach.\n        let bytes = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts_mut(bytes, self.slot_size as usize) };"

}

> AGENT

Work for `0.3.0-4` is complete — cargo cycle clean, 6/6 tests pass, and **Miri now passes clean** on all non-threaded tests (it failed before the fix). Please review. What changed in `src/lib.rs`:

**Fix 1 — Stacked Borrows (Miri-confirmed → Miri-clean):** `init` takes `region.as_mut_ptr()` exactly once into a `base` local; `header` and `slots` both derive from it. The header reference is now `&Header` (shared) since every field is atomic — no `&mut` needed.

**Fix 2 — 32-bit overflow:** `region_size` computes in `u64` (max geometry is ~2^63, can't wrap); `init`/`attach` compare region length as `u64`. A tripwire test (`N = 2^26, M = 2^6`, the product that wraps a 32-bit usize) pins the behavior.

**Fix 3 — attach handshake:** all `Header` fields are now `AtomicU32` (identical layout, so `layout_version` stays 1). `init` stores geometry and indices Relaxed, then `magic` **last with Release**; `attach` loads magic **first with Acquire** — a peer that sees valid magic provably sees the geometry. Geometry is snapshotted into `Ring`/`Producer`/`Consumer` at construction, so hot paths never re-read peer-writable fields — a hostile peer can now cause garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, but not UB. `reserve`'s full check hardened to `>=`.

**Tests added:** `indices_survive_u32_wrap` (starts indices at `u32::MAX - 1`, fills/drains across the wrap — proving the power-of-two wrap argument) and the overflow tripwire. Attach assertions moved to the snapshot fields.

Also noted for the record: the `> 1 << 31` arm in `validate_geometry` now carries a comment explaining it's unreachable-but-documenting, and `check_type`/`slot_ptr` take the snapshot values instead of the header.

The commit description is ready whenever you want it; `-5` (design-doc sync) follows.

> AGENT

Agreed — with one nuance that shapes the helper: the two sites can share the *pointer-level* checks (alignment + room for the `Header` itself + the cast), but not the *full-size* check, because init knows the geometry from its parameters while attach only learns it by reading the header the helper hands back. And the `unsafe` deref should stay at each call site, because the safety justification genuinely differs (init: exclusive borrow; attach: caller's shared-mapping contract). So the getter returns `*const Header` — validated but not yet dereferenced — exactly as you suggested.

One subtlety to preserve: the Misaligned-vs-TooSmall error order. The misaligned test region (`&mut r.0[1..]`) is also one byte short, so the helper must run *before* init's full-size check to keep returning `Misaligned` there. Implementing:

129	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;

> DEVELOPER

Thought, can/should init, attach and split be FnOnce?

> AGENT

Short answer: `FnOnce` is the wrong lever, but the property you're reaching for — *use at most once* — is real, and the code already encodes it everywhere the type system is able to. Let me separate the threads:

**Why not literally `FnOnce`:** every function item in Rust already implements `FnOnce` — vacuously. Calling through `FnOnce` consumes the *closure value*, and function items are `Copy`, so nothing is actually spent; you can call them forever. `FnOnce` constrains values you call, not call sites. The idiomatic way to get call-once semantics is consuming a **non-Copy token** — a `fn foo(self)` — which is affinity, not the `Fn` traits.

**Measured against that, the current API:**

- **`split`** — already exactly this: it consumes `Ring` by value, so one `Producer`+`Consumer` pair per `Ring`, statically. Same for the guards: `commit(self)` / `release(self)` are the consuming-token pattern too — a guard can be committed at most once, checked at compile time.
- **`init`** — the `&'a mut [u8]` gives you once-*at-a-time*: while the returned `Ring` (and its endpoints) live, the region is exclusively borrowed and can't be re-initialized. Once everything drops, re-init is *deliberately* possible — that's ring reset, a feature. "Once ever per region" isn't expressible (a region is just memory, and memory outlives any token we mint for it) and isn't what we want anyway.
- **`attach`** — here the type system is structurally powerless, and no `FnOnce`/affine trick can save it: the input is a `Copy` raw pointer, and more fundamentally, the *second* caller we're worried about is in another process — outside this compiler's jurisdiction entirely. Cross-process once-ness is a protocol property, not a type property.

**The actual fix for attach** is the one sitting in our open-questions list: an endpoint-claims word in the header — attach (or split) does a CAS to claim the producer or consumer role, and a second claimant gets `Error::RoleClaimed`. That gives runtime once-ness that works across processes *and* catches in-process double-attach, which the current documented-contract approach only politely requests. It costs one more line-0 word (layout_version bump, or spend some `_pad0`).

So my take: no API change from the `FnOnce` angle — `split`/guards already have the strongest form, `init` has the right form. But your instinct points squarely at the claims-word design; I'd promote it from open question to a `## Todo` entry (hardening cycle, alongside typed endpoints) during `-5`. Want me to include that?

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/ring-buffer-design.md",
  "old_string": "```rust\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct Header {\n    // line 0 — immutable after init\n    magic: u32,          // layout marker, rejects foreign regions\n    layout_version: u32, // bumped on any layout change\n    slot_size: u32,      // N in bytes, cache-line multiple\n    capacity: u32,       // M, power of two; index mask is M - 1\n    _pad0: [u8; 48],\n    // line 1 — producer-owned\n    producer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by producer only\n    _pad1: [u8; 60],\n    // line 2 — consumer-owned\n    consumer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by consumer only\n    _pad2: [u8; 60],\n}\n```\n\n```\noffset 0                    Header (192 bytes, 3 cache lines)\noffset size_of::<Header>()  slots: M × N bytes\n```\n\n- One type owns the whole control-block layout:\n  `size_of::<Header>()` is the slot-array offset, and a\n  single zerocopy derive covers validation.\n- **Slots** — `M` slots of `N` bytes, each cache-line\n  aligned (N is a line multiple, so alignment follows from\n  the array base).",
  "new_string": "```rust\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct Header {\n    // line 0 — geometry; written by init (magic last), then\n    // read-only\n    magic: AtomicU32,          // layout marker; init/attach handshake\n    layout_version: AtomicU32, // bumped on any layout change\n    slot_size: AtomicU32,      // N in bytes, cache-line multiple\n    capacity: AtomicU32,       // M, power of two; index mask is M - 1\n    _pad0: [u8; 48],\n    // line 1 — producer-owned\n    producer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by producer only\n    _pad1: [u8; 60],\n    // line 2 — consumer-owned\n    consumer_idx: AtomicU32, // written by consumer only\n    _pad2: [u8; 60],\n}\n```\n\n```\noffset 0                    Header (192 bytes, 3 cache lines)\noffset size_of::<Header>()  slots: M × N bytes\n```\n\n- One type owns the whole control-block layout:\n  `size_of::<Header>()` is the slot-array offset.\n- **Every field is atomic** — the region may be mapped by a\n  peer at any time, so even \"immutable\" geometry must be free\n  of data races; a peer scribbling on plain fields would be\n  UB, on atomics it is merely garbage values. `AtomicU32` has\n  the same layout as `u32`, so layout_version is unaffected.\n- **Init/attach handshake** — init stores the geometry and\n  indices first (`Relaxed`), then `magic` **last** with\n  `Release`; attach loads `magic` **first** with `Acquire`. A\n  peer that pre-mapped the region and observes MAGIC therefore\n  also observes the geometry it validates. (Re-initializing a\n  region a peer is already attached to stays outside the\n  contract.)\n- **Slots** — `M` slots of `N` bytes, each cache-line\n  aligned (N is a line multiple, so alignment follows from\n  the array base).\n- **`CACHE_LINE = 64` plays two roles** — a layout-ABI\n  constant (baked into layout_version 1, frozen) and a\n  false-sharing pad. On CPUs with 128-byte effective line\n  pitch (e.g. some aarch64) the padding under-separates; a\n  future layout_version could widen it without touching the\n  ABI role."
}
```

{

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/ring-buffer-design.md",
  "old_string": "## API sketch\n\nConstruction (resolves the foreign-memory question):\n\n- `Ring::init(region: &mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:\n  u32) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` — writes the header, zeroes\n  indices; validates geometry (power-of-two M, line-multiple\n  N, region big enough, alignment).\n- `Ring::attach(region: &[u8]) -> Result<&Ring, Error>` —\n  validated cast of an already-initialized region (an mmap'd\n  segment): checks magic, layout_version, geometry against\n  the region length. Built on zerocopy's `KnownLayout`\n  validated reference casts — no `unsafe` at the call site.\n- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — the two\n  endpoint handles; each is `Send + !Sync` and exists at\n  most once per ring per process (SPSC discipline;\n  cross-process there is one producer process and one\n  consumer process by contract).\n\nProducer — reserve/commit, in place:\n\n- `producer.reserve::<T>() -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>`\n  — next free slot as `&mut T` (zerocopy `FromBytes +\n  IntoBytes + KnownLayout`); `T` geometry checked against\n  `slot_size` once.\n- `Reserved::commit(self)` — publishes the slot\n  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit\n  abandons the reservation (nothing published).",
  "new_string": "## API\n\nConstruction (resolves the foreign-memory question):\n\n- `Ring::init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:\n  u32) -> Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates geometry\n  (power-of-two M, line-multiple N, alignment, region big\n  enough — sized in u64, since the N×M product can wrap a\n  32-bit usize), then writes the header per the\n  [handshake](#memory-layout) (indices zeroed, magic last).\n- `unsafe Ring::attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) ->\n  Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates magic (Acquire),\n  layout_version, and geometry against the region length,\n  over the same shared `header_ptr` checks as init. Unsafe,\n  deliberately, on two grounds:\n  - zerocopy's shared validated casts require `Immutable`,\n    which `Header` correctly is not (atomics) — so the cast\n    is hand-verified rather than derive-verified;\n  - only the caller can vouch that the pointer is a live,\n    genuinely shared, writable mapping (e.g. `MAP_SHARED`)\n    and that the SPSC role contract holds across processes.\n- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — consumes\n  the `Ring`, so each handle exists at most once per ring per\n  process (SPSC discipline; cross-process there is one\n  producer process and one consumer process by contract).\n- **Geometry snapshot** — slot_size / capacity / mask are\n  copied into `Ring` and the handles at init/attach; per-op\n  paths never re-read peer-writable header fields. Combined\n  with the all-atomic header, a hostile peer can degrade the\n  ring to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty — never UB.\n\nProducer — reserve/commit, in place:\n\n- `producer.reserve::<T>() -> Result<Reserved<'_, T>, Full>`\n  — next free slot as `&mut T` (zerocopy `FromBytes +\n  IntoBytes + KnownLayout`); `T` geometry is asserted against\n  the slot size on each call (a mismatch is a programming\n  error, so it panics; typed endpoints that check once are a\n  follow-on — see `## Todo`).\n- `Reserved::commit(self)` — publishes the slot\n  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit\n  abandons the reservation (nothing published)."
}
```

Document as: the ring is safe against UB,\n  not against a malicious peer.\n- Whether `split()` needs a runtime once-guard in the header\n  (endpoint-claimed flags) or stays a documented contract.",
  "new_string": "Batch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —\nthe guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the\nsingle-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.\n\n## Validation\n\nPer-commit, alongside the cargo cycle:\n\n- `cargo +nightly miri test -- --skip threaded` — Miri caught\n  a real Stacked Borrows violation in `init` (0.3.0-4); the\n  non-threaded suite runs clean and stays in the ladder. The\n  threaded stress test is skipped (100k spin-loop iterations\n  under an interpreter).\n- `indices_survive_u32_wrap` pins the free-running-index wrap\n  argument (fill/drain across `u32::MAX` with masked\n  positions intact).\n- [loom](https://docs.rs/loom) for exhaustive ordering\n  exploration is a possible follow-on (Ideas).\n\n## Resolved questions\n\n- **Cross-process trust** — resolved in 0.3.0-4: the\n  all-atomic header plus the geometry snapshot mean a hostile\n  peer cannot cause UB, only garbage messages (still valid\n  `T`s — zerocopy guarantees representation, not sense) or\n  spurious Full/Empty. `attach` remains `unsafe` for the\n  mapping-liveness contract, not for peer trust.\n- **`split()` once-guard** — direction chosen: an\n  endpoint-claims word in the header (CAS to claim the\n  producer / consumer role, second claimant gets an error),\n  which also catches in-process double-attach; costs a\n  layout_version bump (or spends `_pad0`). Promoted to a\n  `## Todo` entry in [todo.md](todo.md); the documented\n  contract stands until then."
}

> AGENT

Work for `0.3.0-5` is complete. Please review. What changed:

**`notes/ring-buffer-design.md`** — synced to as-built:
- Intro now says the doc is kept in sync with `src/lib.rs` rather than promising a future prototype.
- *Safety requirement* scoped honestly: message path fully safe; `attach` is the one unsafe entry, with a pointer to why.
- *Memory layout*: `Header` block shows all-atomic fields; new bullets for why every field is atomic (peer-mapped at any time — races on plain fields would be UB), the magic-last/Acquire-first **init/attach handshake**, and the dual role of `CACHE_LINE = 64` (frozen layout-ABI constant vs false-sharing pad, with the aarch64 128-byte caveat).
- *Index scheme*: full check documented as `>= M` fail-toward-Full hardening.
- *API section* (renamed from "API sketch"): real signatures — `init` with u64 sizing rationale, `unsafe attach(*mut u8, len)` with the two-ground justification (zerocopy `Immutable` doesn't fit an atomic header; mapping liveness is caller-vouched), `split` consuming `Ring`, and the geometry-snapshot guarantee. The per-call type assert is documented with typed endpoints as follow-on.
- New *Validation* section: Miri (non-threaded) joins the per-commit ladder — recording that it caught the real `init` bug — plus the wrap test and loom as a possible follow-on.
- *Open questions* → *Resolved questions*: trust model (resolved by 0.3.0-4; hostile peer ⇒ garbage or spurious Full/Empty, never UB) and the split once-guard (direction: CAS claims word, promoted to Todo).

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores/chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; as a chores file grows
9	large the next one is opened ([chores/chores-01.md](chores/chores-01.md)
10	is the active one in this template), and earlier ones are closed and
11	kept for history.

20	how and why of a particular chore.

```
1	# zc-ring-x1
2	
3	Zero copy ring buffer experiment 1
4	
5	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
6	a bot to help develop a the code.
7	
8	The beginnings of a tool to help is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
9	
10	## Releasing
11	
12	**`cargo install` lockfile gotcha.** If you install your project's
13	binary with `cargo install --path .`, also pass `--locked`. By
14	default `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock` and re-resolves
15	dependencies from scratch, so it can silently build the binary from
16	a different — possibly broken — dependency graph than `cargo build`
17	/ `cargo test` ran against.
18	
19	| command | reads `Cargo.lock`? | re-resolves? |
20	| --- | --- | --- |
21	| `cargo build` / `test` / `run`  | yes     | only if `Cargo.toml` changed |
22	| `cargo build --locked` (etc.)   | yes     | never; errors if it would need to |
23	| `cargo install` (default)       | **no**  | always, from scratch |
24	| `cargo install --locked`        | yes     | never; errors if it would need to |
25	
26	There's no stable cargo config knob to make `--locked` the default
27	for `cargo install`, so the discipline lives in the per-commit cargo
28	cycle and shell aliases (e.g. `alias ci='cargo install --locked'`).
29	
30	The trade-off is predictable-as-tested (`--locked`) vs picking up
31	upstream fixes via fresh resolves (default). Either is defensible —
32	pick what fits your project, then edit this section and the
33	per-commit cargo cycle to match. Background:
34	[cargo#7169](https://github.com/rust-lang/cargo/issues/7169),
35	[cargo#9436](https://github.com/rust-lang/cargo/issues/9436).
36	
37	## jj Tips for Git Users
38	
39	This project uses [Jujutsu (jj)](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/)
40	alongside git. New to jj? See
41	[Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)'s
42	[Jujutsu tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial).
43	
44	Repo-specific how-tos — initial commit, pushing, modifying and
45	force-pushing a commit, revsets, and a useful-commands reference —
46	live in [notes/jj-tips.md](notes/jj-tips.md).
47	
48	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
49	
50	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
51	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo via an
52	`ochid` (Other Change ID) trailer — the defining mechanism of the
53	dual-repo convention. For the full definition — trailer syntax,
54	the example shape, per-commit mechanics, and `.vc-config.toml` —
55	see
56	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers).

69	  shape.

  "old_string": "# zc-ring-x1\n\nZero copy ring buffer experiment 1\n\nThis is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using\na bot to help develop a the code.\n\nThe beginnings of a tool to help is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)",
  "new_string": "# zc-ring-x1\n\nZero copy ring buffer experiment 1\n\nThis is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using\na bot to help develop the code.\n\nThe beginnings of a tool to help is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)\n\n## Overview\n\nA `no_std` SPSC (single-producer / single-consumer) ring buffer\nover a caller-provided memory region, moving typed messages with\nzero copying at the boundary — the producer writes through a\n`&mut T` directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads through\na `&T` in place. Typed views are validated casts via\n[zerocopy](https://docs.rs/zerocopy); the same `#[repr(C)]`\nlayout works between threads and, via shared memory, between\nprocesses. The full design — requirements, memory layout, index\nscheme, API, validation — lives in\n[notes/ring-buffer-design.md](notes/ring-buffer-design.md), kept\nin sync with `src/lib.rs`.\n\n- M slots × N bytes, both caller-chosen: M a power of two, N a\n  cache-line multiple, every slot cache-line aligned.\n- Free-running `AtomicU32` indices, masked only at slot access;\n  no sacrificial slot; lock-free with Acquire/Release pairing.\n- Guard API: `reserve::<T>()` → write → `commit()` on the\n  producer side; `peek::<T>()` → read → `release()` on the\n  consumer side; dropping a guard abandons cleanly.\n- IPC-ready: one contiguous region, no internal pointers, an\n  init/attach handshake (magic published last, Release), and an\n  all-atomic header so a misbehaving peer can corrupt data but\n  never cause UB.\n- Validated by tests (including a threaded stress and a\n  u32-index-wrap proof) and [Miri](https://github.com/rust-lang/miri)\n  on the non-threaded suite.\n\n```rust\nuse zc_ring_x1::Ring;\nuse zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};\n\n#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable)]\n#[repr(C)]\nstruct Msg {\n    seq: u64,\n    val: u64,\n}\n\n// Cache-line-aligned region: 192 B header + 4 slots × 64 B.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct Region([u8; 448]);\nlet mut region = Region([0; 448]);\n\nlet (mut producer, mut consumer) =\n    Ring::init(&mut region.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();\n\nlet mut slot = producer.reserve::<Msg>().unwrap();\nslot.seq = 1;\nslot.val = 42;\nslot.commit(); // publish to the consumer\n\nlet msg = consumer.peek::<Msg>().unwrap();\nassert_eq!(msg.val, 42);\nmsg.release(); // slot is free for reuse\n```\n\nStatus: an experiment. SPSC only; attaching to an existing\nshared-memory region is `unsafe` (see `Ring::attach`); planned\nhardening and follow-ons are tracked in\n[notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md)."
}
```

> AGENT

That's a **real second bug, and your full-suite run caught what my `--skip threaded` run structurally couldn't** — the violation only manifests when both threads race, which is exactly the test I was skipping. Good catch. Let me explain what Miri found, then fix it properly.

**The bug:** `Reserved` holds `msg: &'p mut T` — a live Rust reference into the slot. When you call `slot.commit()`, the guard is passed *by value*, and Stacked Borrows puts a **protector** on the reference fields of that argument for the whole duration of `commit`. Inside that protected window, `commit` does the Release store of `producer_idx` — at which point the consumer thread may immediately Acquire-observe it and read the slot. That read is a foreign access to memory covered by a strongly-protected `&mut` → UB. `Peeked`/`release` has the mirror-image problem (producer may *write* the slot while `release`'s protected `&T` is still live). It's not a Miri false positive — a protected `&mut` is `noalias` to LLVM, so this is genuinely miscompilable. My review point about "the MutexGuard pattern" was wrong in one detail: `MutexGuard` holds a raw pointer to the data, not a `&mut T` field — that's precisely the fix here.

**Fix:** guards store raw pointers (`*mut T` / `*const T`) plus `PhantomData` for the borrow; `Deref`/`DerefMut` mint short-lived references on access (dead by the time `commit`/`release` runs, enforced by borrowck since `commit(self)` moves the guard). This also lets `reserve`/`peek` derive the pointer raw-to-raw from the slot base — the intermediate `&mut [u8]` slice and the zerocopy prefix-cast unwraps disappear; the zerocopy trait bounds stay as the validity proof. And I'll make the threaded test Miri-runnable (`COUNT = 200` under `cfg(miri)`) so the *full* suite runs under Miri from now on — no `--skip`.

This is a code fix, so it shouldn't ride the close-out commit (bookkeeping only). I'm inserting **`-6 fix: ring buffer guard aliasing under Miri`** before close-out: I'll temporarily restore the In Progress block, land `-6`, then redo the close-out edits. Implementing:
